## 2026-10-16 — Safety net (13.1)

**Time spent:** about 1.8 hours (09:56 to 11:42 UTC on 16 Oct; three verify runs of about 15
minutes each are part of it).

**Result:** `VERIFIED tree=cdee41b551f4d9c1 e2e=true`: typecheck, lint, the production build,
4,890 tests, 91 browser tests, the walkthrough's 3, and the invariants all passed. Auditor:
`VERDICT: DONE` (round 3, after two NOT DONE rounds). The work was merged with PR #6; the tick
goes in the next PR.

### What was built

The **safety net** is the set of checks that stop anyone (Claude included) from calling a task
done when it isn't. You approved fixing the gaps an earlier audit found ("How the build is run" 1
to 4). In plain words:

- **verify.sh** (the script that runs every check and writes a **stamp**, a small file saying
  "these exact files passed") now takes the files' **fingerprint** (a short code worked out from
  their contents) when it starts. If any file changes while it runs, there's no stamp. It also
  runs the **production build** (the same build the server will run), and it fails if the
  project's extra checks (`scripts/invariants.sh`) are missing.
- **Ticks need the browser tests.** A task can only be ticked if the stamp includes the browser
  tests (`RUN_E2E=1`).
- **The tick guard** (a **hook**: a small script Claude Code runs before or after each action)
  reads each task's own box. Moving a tick from one task to another, or writing `[X]`, is caught.
  It also notices a tick made by a shell command, and then refuses `git commit` and `git push`
  until it's undone.
- **The Stop hook** (it runs when Claude finishes a reply) catches more ways of saying "done"
  ("Phase 0 is done.", "All 2665 tests pass."). It blocks up to 3 times in a row.
- **invariants.sh** now refuses browser tests that are switched off (Playwright's `fixme`,
  `fail` and skips decided while a test runs). It reads the browser tests' own result files too,
  checks `scripts/` for anything setting the firm outside `withFirm()`, and refuses a test that
  imports the Twilio library.
- **The reviewers and the auditor are read-only.** A hook refuses any edit from them, and any
  shell command that could write a file or run the project. It reads each command the way the
  shell does: quotes, escapes, comments, variables, `$(…)`. So `sed "-i"` counts as `sed -i`, and
  options that write (`sort -o`, `uniq in out`, a sed script with `w`) are refused.

### Decisions made

| Decision | Why | What else was considered |
|---|---|---|
| A shell tick is reported after the command, not undone for you | Undoing it could lose work in a stash, merge or reset | Rewriting `PLAN.md` back automatically |
| The read-only check refuses whatever it can't read for certain (variables, `$(…)`, loops) | A variable's value could turn into an option that writes; refusing is safe, and the reviewers can run one plain command at a time | Allowing them and trying to guess the value |
| `less`, `more`, `xxd` and `file` left off the reviewers' command list | Each can run something or write in some mode, and none is needed to read code | Checking each one's options |
| The tests run verify.sh with stand-in commands | Running the real tests inside the tests would take 15 minutes and loop | Only reading verify.sh's text |

### What the new tests prove

`tests/unit/safety-net.test.ts` has 34 tests. Each runs the real hook or script as a separate
program in a throwaway copy of a repository:

- every way of ticking (an edit, a moved tick, `[X]`, a shell command);
- the Stop hook;
- about 100 reviewer commands, allowed and refused;
- `invariants.sh` on sample folders, each refusal with a passing case;
- `verify.sh` with stand-in commands: the build runs, a file changed mid-run gives NOT VERIFIED,
  and so does a missing or non-executable `invariants.sh`;
- the hooks wired to the right actions in `.claude/settings.json`.

On top of that, the scripts were broken on purpose 13 ways, and a test failed every time.

### What's parked or needs Dylan

- **Parked:** a single shell command that ticks `PLAN.md` and commits in one go gets its commit in
  before the warning. The tick is still reported, and the next commit or push is refused. It's in
  the Parking lot under "The shell-tick watch is after the fact".

### How to see it working

Run `bash scripts/selftest.sh`. It breaks each rule on purpose and shows the hook refusing it,
ending with "SELFTEST PASSED".

## 2026-10-16 — The shifts line's last try (13.2)

**Time spent:** about 1.1 hours (11:43 to 12:49 UTC on 16 Oct, a container restart included).

**Result:** `VERIFIED tree=c4449b781a9135a0 e2e=true`: typecheck, lint, the production build,
4,901 tests, 91 browser tests, the walkthrough's 3, and the invariants all passed. Auditor:
`VERDICT: DONE` (first round). The work was merged with PR #7; the tick goes in the next PR.

### What was built

The **shifts line** answers casuals' WhatsApps and texts by itself, in a **job** (work done in the
background, after the message is stored). If the job fails, it's tried again. Before this task, a
message whose job failed every time was never answered and never shown to anyone.

Now:

- A failed answer is tried again 3 times, 2 seconds apart. The last try comes after about 15
  seconds of waiting.
- On the **last try**, the message is passed to a person: it shows in **Need a person** on
  Enquiries, with "The automatic answer failed on every try (TypeError): passed to a person." The
  action log says the same. Nothing is sent to the casual; the office answers.
- The job still counts as **failed**, so the alert for failed jobs tells you. The failure is always
  in our code (unlike a firm's own planner webhook, which completes quietly).
- A **STOP** or **START** text still counts on the last try: texts are turned off or on, exactly
  as normal, just without the confirmation text.
- Only a short **code** for the error is kept (like `TypeError` or `57P01`), never its text.

### Decisions made

| Decision | Why | What else was considered |
|---|---|---|
| The job still fails after passing the message on | The guardrails review found the first version hid the only alert for a broken shifts line | Completing quietly, like the planner webhooks (their failures are the firm's own) |
| 2 seconds between tries, no doubling | A casual is waiting; doubling waits made it 35 to 70 seconds | pg-boss's doubling waits |
| A STOP uses the same claim as the normal STOP code | One copy of the rules, so the two can't drift apart | Leaving a failed STOP unanswered |
| No reply to the casual on the last try | Not in the task; it's your call (Parking lot) | Sending "the office will get back to you" |

### What the new tests prove

`tests/integration/inbound.last-try.test.ts` has 11 tests on the real database. They cover:

- each try before the last fails, and the last passes the message on once and sends nothing;
- a repeat of the last try, or a later try that works, changes nothing;
- the hand-off waits for another job holding the message, then leaves it alone;
- a STOP still turns texts off;
- a stranger's message and a former casual's message;
- an older message is passed on only by its own job;
- an odd error name shows as "unknown";
- a real background worker retries by itself and ends with the job failed and counted.

13 deliberate breaks of the code were each caught by a test.

### What's parked or needs Dylan

- **Needs you:** should the last try also send the casual the fixed "the office will get back to
  you" reply? (Parking lot, "Needs Dylan: an answer on the shifts line's last try".)
- **Parked:** three rare cases still leave a message unanswered and out of Need a person:
  - a worker killed in the middle of a last try;
  - the hand-off itself failing;
  - jobs that failed before this change.

  Each still raises the failed-job alert. It's in the Parking lot under "Messages stranded".

### How to see it working

`npm test -- tests/integration/inbound.last-try.test.ts` runs the 11 tests. On a running app you'd
see it only when something is broken: the message appears in Need a person with the reason.

## 2026-10-16 — The web push sender (13.3)

**Time spent:** about 1.8 hours (12:49 to 14:35 UTC on 16 Oct).

**Result:** `VERIFIED tree=48e314a921e1e97b e2e=true`: typecheck, lint, the production build,
4,912 tests, 91 browser tests, the walkthrough's 3, and the invariants all passed. Auditor:
`VERDICT: DONE` (first round).

### What was built

A **web push** is the small notification that pops up on a phone's lock screen, sent through the
browser maker's own **push service** (Google for Chrome and Android, Apple for iPhones, Mozilla
for Firefox, Microsoft for Edge). Until now the app only *printed* pushes (the console sender).
This task adds the real sender, still switched off.

- **The sender** uses the `web-push` package. Each push is **encrypted** so only that phone can
  read it, and **signed** with the deployment's **VAPID keys** (a key pair that proves the push
  came from us).
- Only the title, the words and the link go to the phone.
- A push the phone hasn't picked up is kept for 12 hours at most, or until the shift starts
  (offers and reminders). Urgent offers go at high urgency.
- A push that hasn't finished in 5 seconds is given up on, and its connection is closed. A worker's
  phones are pushed at the same time, not one after another.
- **404 or 410** from the push service means the phone's subscription is gone: the app forgets it.
  Any other failure keeps the phone and logs the firm, the push service's host and a short code,
  never the phone's address.
- **Push addresses** are only saved in the exact form a browser gives, and only for the four
  browser makers' push services. The sender checks again before every push.
- **Saving a phone** (`POST /api/push/subscribe`) is limited to 10 saves every 15 minutes per
  casual (then "429, try again in …"). Re-saving the same phone and turning notifications off are
  never refused.
- **Settings:** `PUSH_TRANSPORT=webpush` needs all three keys, as a matching pair. It's refused in
  tests, and in production too until one more build step is done (below). The worker alone gets
  the private key.
- **Docs:** the push services are listed as sub-processors (`docs/compliance.md`), with what a
  locked phone shows, in the privacy notice too.

### Decisions made

| Decision | Why | What else was considered |
|---|---|---|
| Only the exact address form a browser gives | The guardrails review found `https://attacker.example%2Epush.apple.com/x`: our check read it as Apple, but web-push would have connected to `attacker.example` | Decoding and re-checking the address |
| Production refuses `webpush` for now | Pushes still go inside the database transaction of the record they follow; a slow push service would hold it open | Switching on with the 5-second cut-off only |
| A save that changes nothing isn't counted | The first version's limit could stop a casual turning notifications off | Counting every request |
| A worker's phones pushed at once | Three phones one after another could hold a transaction for 15 seconds | One after another |
| Forget a phone only on 404 or 410 | A 403 means our keys are wrong, not the phone | Forgetting on any failure |

### What the new tests prove

- `tests/unit/push.webpush.test.ts` (7 tests) runs web-push's own sender over real HTTPS to a
  **fake push service on this machine** (with a certificate made for the test). It checks the
  signature with our public key, decrypts the push with the phone's keys, and checks the 12-hour
  limit and urgency. It also covers the 201, 410, 404 and 403 answers, and a service that never
  finishes (cut off at the deadline, connection closed). Refused addresses are never contacted.
- `tests/integration/push.test.ts` (8 tests) covers saving: the address and key checks and the
  limit. It also covers pushing: all at once, a failed push kept and logged, gone or bad addresses
  forgotten.
- `tests/e2e/push.worker.spec.ts` sends the route nine 200s, then the 429, in a real browser. A CI
  retry starts fresh: running it twice on one database passes, and fails without the reset.
- No test reaches a real push service: the test settings pin the console sender, and
  `scripts/invariants.sh` checks it.

Three guardrails rounds: 2 blocking findings in the first (the address trick, and the limit), none
after. Each round's fixes were broken on purpose to check a test catches them. In the last round,
one gap turned up: the length check was only covered by the host list. It now has its own test.

### What's parked or needs Dylan

- **Before web push is switched on** (Claude): push after the record's transaction, not inside
  it. PLAN.md, "Connect the real web push" step 0.
- **Needs the server:** making the VAPID keys and testing on real phones. Steps 1 to 4 of the same
  Parking lot item.

### How to see it working

`npm test -- tests/unit/push.webpush.test.ts tests/integration/push.test.ts` runs the push tests.
Nothing changes on screen yet: the profile still says phone notifications aren't switched on.

## 2026-10-16 — Privacy switches (13.4)

**Time spent:** about 1.4 hours (14:29 to 15:52 UTC on 16 Oct).

**Result:** `VERIFIED tree=9829a293a6d0ae1a e2e=true`: typecheck, lint, the production build,
4,927 tests, 92 browser tests, the walkthrough's 3, and the invariants all passed. Auditor:
`VERDICT: DONE` (first round).

### What was built

Two privacy changes you decided on 16 October.

**1. Travel areas are switched on per firm.** A **travel area** is a casual's start postcode and
how far they'll travel. The app uses it to show only shifts they can reach. A firm's own **DPIA**
(the data protection check it must sign first) is per firm, so the switch is too:
`travel.enabled`, set by the owner with `npm run firm:settings`. It's off for every new firm. The
demo firm, Acme, has it on.

When it's off:

- A casual sees no "Where you work" on their profile, and its page isn't found. Saving one is
  refused, even if the switch changes while the postcode is being looked up.
- No "Add your home area" prompt, no travel lines ("3.1 miles · 12 min by car"), no "further than
  you travel" line, and no help section about it.
- The shift page's "Getting there" keeps only the "Open in maps" button.
- Nothing is filtered or sorted by distance: Open shifts, the urgent board, Home, the shifts
  line's reply and offer waves all treat everyone as if they had no area.
- No travel times are worked out for that firm. A run already going stops before its next group.
- The office's Casuals page has no "Travel area" column or card. A message's checks say "Travel
  areas are off for this firm: not filtered by distance ✓".
- Home areas saved before are kept but not used (see "Needs Dylan" below).

**2. Payroll's note stays behind the pay code.** When payroll sorts a pay query, the casual's
message used to include payroll's note. Now it says: "Payroll has answered your pay query (ref
3F2A9C1B). See their answer on your pay page: {link} — Acme Events Shift Team". The note is only
on the pay page, which needs the 6-digit pay code. Payroll's own screen and guide now say so.

### Decisions made

| Decision | Why | What else was considered |
|---|---|---|
| Off unless the setting is exactly `true` | Off is the private side: a typo or a missing setting can't switch it on | Treating any "truthy" value as on |
| The office's travel column and card hidden too when off | The data isn't used, so it shouldn't be shown | Showing districts that no longer do anything |
| Saved home areas kept when switched off | Not in the task, and it's your call | Deleting them on switch-off (parked) |
| The pay message carries the pay page's link | Every other message gives a link; the casual knows where to go | Words only, no link |

### What the new tests prove

- **Both switch states**, in each place: the lists, the urgent board, Home, booked shifts, the
  shift page, the help page, the profile, the shifts line's reply and its log entry, offer waves,
  the travel-time job and its nightly run, and the office's pages. Most "off" tests switch a firm
  off, check, then switch it back on and see the old behaviour return.
- **A browser test** switches the demo firm off, checks a casual's pages, and puts it back.
- **The pay message** is checked word for word, and no part of the note may appear in it (the
  integration test, the template-doc test and two browser tests).
- 24 deliberate breaks of the new code were each caught by a test.

### Something else that came up

CI's `verify` job failed once, on a planner webhook test this task didn't touch. **Root cause:**
the test stopped its job worker with `offWork(queue, { id })`. Passing any options replaces
pg-boss's default `{ wait: true }`, so it didn't wait for the job in hand. The test then read the
job's state while pg-boss was still finishing it ("active", not "completed"). A slower runner
showed it. I reproduced it by adding a delay after the job's handler (it failed the same way),
then fixed it with `{ id, wait: true }` (the same slowed run passed).

### What's parked or needs Dylan

- **Needs you:** when a firm switches travel areas off, should the home areas already saved be
  deleted? Today they're kept, unused, and the casual has no button to remove theirs while it's
  off. (PLAN.md Parking lot, "Travel areas", Needs Dylan (13.4).)
- **Before submitting the WhatsApp templates:** `pay_query_update`'s new wording and variables
  (ref, link, signOff) are what goes to Meta.

### How to see it working

On the demo (`npm run dev`, then `http://acme.localhost:3000`), travel areas are on as before. To
see them off, as the owner: `npm run firm:settings -- --firm acme --set travel.enabled=false`; a
casual's profile then has no "Where you work", and Open shifts no travel lines. Switch back with
`--set travel.enabled=true`.

## 2026-10-16 — Tidy-ups (13.5)

**Time spent:** about 1.5 hours (15:44 to 16:01, then 18:38 to 19:52 UTC on 16 Oct; the container
restarted in between and those 2.6 hours aren't counted).

**Result:** `VERIFIED tree=366cd407441f52b5 e2e=true`: typecheck, lint, the production build,
4,942 tests, 92 browser tests, the walkthrough's 3, and the invariants all passed. Auditor:
`VERDICT: DONE` (first round), with Phase 13's whole "Done when" line checked.

### What was built

Eight small items, most from your decisions of 16 October.

1. **The 24-hour reminder** says "Can't make it? Cancel with the link as early as you can." (There's
   no app to download.)
2. **Logging in on venue Wi-Fi.** Everyone on one Wi-Fi shares one internet address, and only 10
   login links per address every 15 minutes were allowed. Now 60, enough for a briefing of about
   50. The limits per phone number (3 every 15 minutes, 10 a day) are unchanged.
3. **Cutting long text.** A WhatsApp, text or voicemail over 1,600 characters is cut. The old cut
   counted the way JavaScript does, where an emoji is two "units", so it could cut one in half and
   store "�". A new shared helper (`src/lib/text/cut.ts`) only cuts between whole characters as a
   person sees them (a flag or an emoji with its skin tone stays whole). It's used at both
   webhooks, the voicemail, the AI's input, and four other places that shorten text.
4. **Open shifts' rules for the whole list at once:** already built in Phase 10 (one rules check
   for the list, the same number of database queries for 5 shifts as for 40). Its test now runs
   with travel areas on, so it counts those reads too.
5. **"Take shift" limit:** 20 taps per casual every 15 minutes, booked or not. After that: "Too
   many tries. Try again in {n} minutes." It's logged once. This stops one phone hammering the
   rules and the action log.
6. **Licence expiry messages** now go by the channel the casual chose (WhatsApp or text), through
   the same sender as everything else. If WhatsApp refuses, a text goes instead. A casual who
   replied STOP gets WhatsApp only. Before, these went by WhatsApp whatever they chose.
7. **The flaky sheets test** was already fixed on 6 October. There was no test that would notice
   if that fix were undone, so I added one.
8. **A leaner server image.** The image the server runs no longer carries the development tools
   (the linter, the test runners, TypeScript's compiler): 2.22 GB down to 1.73 GB on a local build.
   `tsx` (which runs the worker and the database migrations) is now a normal dependency. The build
   itself fails if `tsx` can't compile a file, or a test tool is left in.

### Decisions made

| Decision | Why | What else was considered |
|---|---|---|
| 60 login links per address | Covers a briefing of about 50; the per-number limits stop flooding one phone | A per-firm setting (parked as an option) |
| 20 taps per casual on "Take shift", counting every tap | Plenty for real use; simple to explain | Counting only refused taps |
| Cut by whole visible characters | No "�", and a flag never loses half | Cutting by code point only |
| Event names left cut by code point | They must match the same name built in the database | Changing both sides (parked) |
| `@playwright/test` left in the image | npm keeps it as Next's optional peer; nothing loads it | Deleting it by hand after the prune |

## 2026-10-19 — Pushes after the record's transaction (14.1)

**Time spent:** about 1.1 hours (10:10 to 11:13 UTC on 19 Oct, three full checks included).

**Result:** `VERIFIED tree=e412caa400540c36 e2e=true`: typecheck, lint, the production build,
4,947 tests, 92 browser tests, the walkthrough's 3, and the invariants all passed. Auditor: `VERDICT: DONE` (first round).

### What was built

A **push** is the free notification that pops up on a casual's phone (like an app's alert), sent
through Google's, Apple's or Mozilla's push service. Every message the app sends a casual (an
offer, a reminder, a booking, a change, an answer) is followed by a push to each phone they turned
notifications on for.

Until now the push was sent while a **database transaction** was open. (A transaction is a small
bundle of database changes that are saved together or not at all. While one is open it holds one
of the worker's database connections. The worker has only 10 to share between all its jobs.) A
push service that was slow to answer could hold a connection for up to 5 seconds per message, and
across an offer wave that adds up. That's why the worker refused to send real pushes in
production.

Now each of the 7 senders works in this order:

1. **Claim** the message in the database (so a retry never sends it twice).
2. **Send** the WhatsApp or text, with no transaction open.
3. **Push** to the casual's phones, with no transaction open. The phones are looked up in a short
   transaction of their own first.
4. **Record** what happened in one short transaction: the message's event, with how many phones
   took the push, and any phone the push service says is gone is forgotten.

The worker now accepts `PUSH_TRANSPORT=webpush` in production. Real pushes still need the keys
made on the server ("Connect the real web push" in PLAN.md's Parking lot).

Key facts:

- **A push can't undo the record of a message that went.** If a push throws an error, even before
  it starts, it counts as a failed push and is logged. If the phones can't be looked up, nothing
  is pushed and "push not tried" is logged. If forgetting a gone phone fails, that's logged and
  kept apart (a savepoint: a mark inside the transaction that lets one part be undone without the
  rest), so the message's record still saves.
- **A phone is only forgotten if it's still the one that was pushed to.** If a casual logs in again
  on that phone while the push is out, the phone gets new keys and is kept.
- **Calling the push with a transaction open is refused at once**, before anything is pushed, so
  a future sender can't slip back into the old way.
- **Nothing that could hold a phone's push address is logged.** An error's name could hold it, so
  only a short safe code is kept ("unknown" otherwise).

### Decisions made

| Decision | Why | What else was considered |
|---|---|---|
| Look the phones up in their own short transaction | One shape for all 7 senders | Reading them in each sender's claim (7 different claims to change) |
| Push before recording, not after | Keeps the push count in the message's event, as before | Recording first, then pushing (the count would need a second write) |
| Forget gone phones in a savepoint | A failed delete can't roll back the message's record | Leaving it as before (a rare deadlock could lose the record) |
| Refuse a call made inside a transaction | Makes "no transaction during a push" true when the app runs, not just in tests | A warning only |

### What the new tests prove

- **Every sender** (offer waves, the office's offer, reminders, booking notices, change notices,
  request answers, pay answers): at the moment each push goes, no transaction is open.
- A push that throws, one that throws before it starts, and one whose error name is the phone's
  address: each is a failed push with a safe code, and the message is still recorded as sent.
- A phone saved again with new keys while the push went is kept; unchanged, it's forgotten.
- A forget that fails is logged, and the transaction it's in still saves.
- Production now accepts webpush (with its keys); tests still refuse it.
- One older reminder test made "the record fails after the message went" happen by making the push
  throw. A push can't do that any more, so the test now makes the database go away instead. It
  still checks the same thing: the reminder stays "sending" and is never sent twice.
- The "never sends twice" tests (`jobs.no-resend`) make the record step fail after a message went,
  then retry. Their fault used to hit "the first transaction after a message", which was the record
  step. Now the push's short lookup comes first, and a failed lookup doesn't fail the job, so four
  of them stopped reaching the record step (the first full check caught it). They now fail every
  transaction after a message, as if the database went away: the record step fails again, and the
  retry still sends nothing.
- 9 deliberate breaks, each caught: pushing inside a transaction again (8 tests fail, one or more
  per sender), no catch on a throwing push, forgetting a phone whatever its keys, a lookup failure
  that throws, the old production refusal, no guard against an open transaction, a push that throws
  before it starts, logging an error's raw name, and forgetting without the savepoint.

### What's parked or needs Dylan

- Nothing new for Dylan. From the review: a job that dies between the push and the record loses the
  message's provider id (as it did before). That matters only once Twilio's delivery reports are
  connected (parked with "Connect Twilio").

### How to see it working

`npm test -- tests/integration/push.test.ts` runs the push's own tests. Real pushes on a phone wait
for the server ("Connect the real web push").

## 2026-10-19 — Login safety and the log's filters (14.2)

**Time spent:** about 0.6 hours (11:13 to 11:49 UTC on 19 Oct).

**Result:** `VERIFIED tree=e2d2a59b61fc5212 e2e=true`: typecheck, lint, the production build,
4,952 tests, 92 browser tests, the walkthrough's 3, and the invariants all passed. Auditor: `VERDICT: DONE` (first round).

### What was built

1. **An alert when a firm sends far more login links than usual.** Since 13.5, one internet
   address may ask for 60 login links every 15 minutes, so a briefing on a venue's Wi-Fi isn't
   locked out. Each link is a paid WhatsApp or text, and nothing watched the total. The worker's
   checks (every 5 minutes) now count each firm's login links:
   - more than **100 in the last hour** sends you an alert ("acme: 101 login links by WhatsApp and
     text in the last hour…");
   - more than **300 in the last 24 hours** sends another, for a slow drip that never reaches 100 in
     an hour (each phone number can get 10 a day, so links to a crew's numbers spread through the
     day could run to hundreds).

   It's an **alert, not a ceiling**. A ceiling would let a handful of known numbers lock a whole
   firm's casuals out on an event morning. The alert says only the firm's short name and a count,
   never a number or a name. Until the phone sender is connected, alerts are a line in the server's
   log ("Alerts from the app" in `docs/runbooks.md`).
2. **The office's action log files each limit by what it limits.** When a limit stops someone, the
   log records it. All of these used to be under "Logins", even "Too many taps on Take shift". Now:
   - too many "Take shift" taps → **Bookings**;
   - too many saves of a phone's notifications → **Workers**;
   - the planner's API key, and the strikes team's confirmation links → **Firm settings and office
     team**;
   - everything else (login links, codes, office links) → **Logins**.

   The Type filter and the label on each line agree.

### Decisions made

| Decision | Why | What else was considered |
|---|---|---|
| An alert, not a firm-wide ceiling | A ceiling could lock every casual out; the per-number limits already cap what one person can be sent | A daily ceiling per firm (the Parking lot's other option) |
| 100 an hour, 300 a day | A briefing of about 50 is the busiest real hour; casuals stay logged in for 30 days | Lower numbers (alerts on normal mornings); one threshold only (misses a slow drip, the review's finding) |
| Count links sent or tried, WhatsApp and text only | Each is a paid message; office links go by email | Counting office email links |
| Strikes team links under Firm settings | Their other events are there (the review's finding) | Leaving them in Logins |

### What the new tests prove

- The alert's thresholds on both sides (100 and 300 don't alert, 101 and 301 do), its exact words,
  its length with the longest firm name, and a firm name that looks like a phone number.
- On the real database: only this firm's links, only WhatsApp and text, only the last hour (or the
  last 24 hours for the day's alert); a slow drip over a day alerts; and the day's links never water
  down the last hour's failure rate.
- Every limit the app has lands in a group, so a new one can't slip in unfiled; on the real database
  each Type shows exactly its limits.
- 11 deliberate breaks, each caught: each threshold one lower, the alert left out, failed links not
  counted, the day's count with no 24-hour edge or only an hour's, the day's links counted into the
  hour's sends, "Take shift" or the strikes links left unfiled, the Logins catch-all left out of the
  filter, and the label ignoring the limit's name.
- The guardrails review found two things worth fixing, both fixed: the strikes team's links were
  filed under Logins, and a slow drip of links (never 100 in an hour) would never alert.

### What's parked or needs Dylan

- The two numbers (100 an hour, 300 a day) are starting points: look again with a pilot firm's real
  mornings.

### How to see it working

`npm test -- tests/integration/ops.alerts.test.ts` and
`npm test -- tests/integration/office.action-log.test.ts`. In the running app: Action log, Type
"Bookings", shows a "Too many taps on Take shift" line once a casual has tapped it 21 times in 15
minutes.

## 2026-10-19 — Small fixes (14.3)

**Time spent:** about 0.5 hours (11:49 to 12:19 UTC on 19 Oct).

**Result:** `VERIFIED tree=20af433f582fa925 e2e=true`: typecheck, lint, the production build,
4,954 tests, 92 browser tests, the walkthrough's 3, and the invariants all passed. Auditor: `VERDICT: DONE` (first round), with Phase 14's whole "Done when" line checked.

### What was built

Three small leftovers from the 13.5 review:

1. **The shifts line's very last reply is now signed.** When a casual asks the shifts line for
   shifts and nothing else fits in a message, the reply falls back to just the link. If even that
   was too long, it used to go without the "Acme Events Shift Team" sign-off, breaking golden rule
   8 (every reply is signed by the firm's Shift Team). Now the text is cut to leave room for the
   sign-off. It can't happen with real names and links, but the rule now holds everywhere.
2. **Automatic event names never split a character.** An event the app makes for a site and a day
   is named "{site} · {day}", and a very long site name is cut to fit the database's 200
   characters. The cut counted single code points, so a flag (two code points) at the cut could
   lose half and show as a broken symbol. It now uses the shared cut from 13.5, which only cuts
   between whole characters. (The same names were once built in the database by migration 0035,
   a one-off that ran over the demo data of the time; nothing builds them there now.)
3. **A licence warning caught up after a crash doesn't guess its channel.** If the expiry scan
   stops between claiming a warning and logging it, the next scan logs it as "unconfirmed". It
   used to record the casual's current channel (WhatsApp or text), which might not be the one it
   went by. The claim doesn't keep that, so now no channel is recorded rather than a guess.

### Decisions made

| Decision | Why | What else was considered |
|---|---|---|
| Sign the last resort by cutting the text before the sign-off | Keeps golden rule 8 with the fewest changes | Keeping the automatic-reply line and cutting only the link (more code for a case that can't happen) |
| Leave migration 0035 as it is | It has run; changing an applied migration rewrites history | Editing it to match |
| No channel rather than the current one | An honest "unknown" beats a possible wrong answer | Storing the channel with the claim (a schema change for a rare case) |

### What the new tests prove

- A link of over 1,700 characters gives a reply within 1,600 characters, on WhatsApp and by text,
  with shifts and without, that still ends with the firm's sign-off.
- A site name with a flag across the cut drops the whole flag; a name that fits keeps it.
- A licence warning caught up after a crash is logged once, unconfirmed, with no channel.
- 5 deliberate breaks, each caught: the last resort unsigned again, or signed with no room left for
  the sign-off; the event name cut by code point again (twice: in the new test file, then in the
  existing one after merging); and the warning's guessed channel put back.
- The review noted my new event-name test file duplicated an existing one: its cases now live in
  `tests/unit/shift-events.test.ts`, whose header no longer says the migration still makes names.

### What's parked or needs Dylan

- From the review (small): an error's name is logged without masking (`src/lib/log/mask.ts`), so an
  error named with a phone number would log it. Parked in PLAN.md.

### How to see it working

`npm test -- tests/unit/shift-events.test.ts` and
`npm test -- tests/unit/inbound.personal-reply.test.ts`.

## 2026-10-20 — Phase 16, task 16.1: demo mode in the app

**Time:** 1.7 hours. **Result:** `RUN_E2E=1 ./scripts/verify.sh` printed
`VERIFIED tree=609d8c1542f3868f e2e=true` (5,037 unit and integration tests, 93 browser tests, and
the walkthrough run's 6). The `claims-auditor` said **DONE** on the first round. Committed and
pushed to the Phase 16 pull request.

### What was built, in plain words

You bought a Hetzner server and a domain so your boss can click through the app online. But the
app has no real text or email sender yet (that's Phase 15), and logging in needs a link sent by
text or email. **Demo mode** (`DEMO_MODE=on`, a server setting) solves that, safely:

1. **Nothing is sent; everything is shown.** Every text and email the app would send is printed
   into a file (the "outbox") instead. Each demo firm has a **Demo phone** page
   (`https://acme.<your domain>/demo`) that lists them, newest first, and checks for new ones every
   4 seconds. Tap a login link there, as you would on a phone. A yellow **demo bar** on every page
   says it's a demo and links to the Demo phone.
2. **Nothing leaves the server.** In demo mode the settings refuse every real sender: texts, email
   and the planner's webhooks are printed; web push, Google Sheets and AI are off; postcodes are the
   made-up ones.
3. **One password for the whole site.** The app itself asks for it (the browser's own password box;
   user name `demo`). It's the app's own check, so it holds whatever sits in front of the app. Only
   the health check, Caddy's certificate check and the planner API (which checks its own key) skip
   it.
4. **Made-up firms and people only.** The job worker (the background program that sends messages)
   refuses to start, and stops within a minute, if the database holds any firm other than the two
   made-up ones, or anyone in them with a real phone number or email address. Made up means:
   numbers in Ofcom's drama range (07700 900000 to 900999) and emails on the example domains. In
   between its checks it refuses any other firm's job, and refuses any message to someone who isn't
   made up. And the web app can't find any other firm at all in demo mode: its address, shifts line
   and planner keys all come up "not found".

Key facts:
- **Each message is stamped with the firm that sent it** (every job now runs inside its firm's
  context, which the printer reads). Each firm's Demo phone shows only its own stamped messages.
  This came from the safety review: a casual can work for two firms with one phone number, so "who
  it went to" can't say which firm sent it.
- The browser tests now run the walkthrough exactly as a demo server will: a production build,
  both programs in production demo mode, behind the password.

### Decisions made

| Decision | Why | What else was considered |
|---|---|---|
| A "Demo phone" page (Dylan's choice) | The real login flow stays as it is; offers, reminders and replies show too | The login link shown on the login page |
| Stamp each outbox line with the sending firm | One number can be in two firms; matching by recipient leaked one firm's texts to the other's page | Matching by recipient only (the first version) |
| The password is the app's own check | Holds even if the server's front door (Caddy) were set up wrong; the browser tests prove it | Only in Caddy |
| In demo mode the app finds no other firm | A check in the page layout can't stop the page under it running (Next's own guide says so) | "Not on this server" in the layout (the first fix; the second review caught it) |
| The planner API skips the password | It has its own key, and a request can't carry both | No planner API on a demo server |

### Fixed on the way

- **A weekday bug in the demo seed's test** (already on `main`): the story books Zoe at a festival
  3 days from today, 14:00 to 22:00, and the walkthrough's urgent dropout is next Saturday at
  08:00. On Tuesdays her shift ends 10 hours before it; on Wednesdays it's the same day. The rules
  rightly stop her taking it (11 hours' rest), but the test assumed she always could, so it failed
  every Tuesday and Wednesday. It now checks the rule both ways, and a new test pins which days
  clash.

### What the new tests prove

- The settings: production refuses printed messages without demo mode, and demo mode refuses every
  real sender; production demo mode needs the password.
- The worker's checks: other firms, real people, three missed checks, and other firms' jobs.
- Isolation: two firms, one number in both, each sees only its own messages (on the real
  database); the app's look-ups find no other firm in demo mode.
- The password: wrong, missing or odd headers refused; the health check and planner API let through.
- In the browser, on the production build: the password is asked everywhere; a casual and the
  office each log in from the Demo phone; neither firm sees the other's messages. And on a normal
  server: no Demo phone and no demo bar.

### What's parked or needs Dylan

- Nothing to do yet: 16.2 (the server's files and the step-by-step guide) comes next, in the same
  pull request. Don't merge until it's ready.

## 2026-10-20 — Phase 16, task 16.2: the demo server

**Time:** 1.1 hours (Phase 16 in all: 2.8 hours). **Result:** `RUN_E2E=1 ./scripts/verify.sh`
printed `VERIFIED tree=9fc30c992f63cd44 e2e=true` (5,071 unit and integration tests, 93 browser
tests, and the walkthrough run's 6). The `claims-auditor` said **DONE** on the first round, checking
the whole phase against its "Done when" line.

### What was built, in plain words

Everything your Hetzner server needs to run the demo from 16.1, plus the step-by-step guide
(`docs/runbooks.md`, "A demo server").

1. **One extra settings file for the server** (`deploy/docker-compose.demo.yml`). It's used together
   with the real one, so the two can't drift apart. It:
   - runs the database (Postgres) on the server itself, reachable only through a file on the server
     (a "socket"), with no network address at all;
   - switches demo mode on, and every sender off or to printing, itself (not left to your settings);
   - shares the outbox between the worker (which writes it) and the web app (which only reads it).
2. **One command loads the demo afresh** (`./demo-reset.sh`), by hand the first time, then every
   night at 04:00 UK time, so the demo's dates move on and whatever visitors did is cleared. It
   deletes and rebuilds the whole demo database, so it refuses unless:
   - it's a demo server (demo mode on, the database on the server itself);
   - it logs in as the database's superuser (the only login that can see every firm);
   - an existing database is one it made itself (it tags the ones it makes) and holds nothing but
     the two made-up firms.
3. **The demo's database refuses real people itself.** A database rule (a "trigger") refuses any
   casual's phone number outside Ofcom's drama range, and any email address outside the example
   domains, however it's written: a page, an import or the planner API. The app can't switch it off.
4. **The site password is the web app's own rule now.** Without it, a production demo server turns
   every visitor away with a message and reports itself unhealthy, so the setup says so.

### How it was checked, beyond the tests

A full **rehearsal in Docker** on this machine: the real image, both settings files, the example
settings filled in, and the guide's own command.
- Every part came up healthy, and the password was asked for.
- A browser logged a casual and the office in from the Demo phone, and Northline's showed nothing
  of Acme's.
- The database refused a real number.
- A firm that isn't a demo firm stopped the worker in 33 seconds, and the reset then refused to
  delete the database.
- The rehearsal also found a real bug before it reached your server: the worker crashed because the
  settings demanded the site password, which only the web app gets. Fixed by moving that rule into
  the web app.

### Decisions made

| Decision | Why | What else was considered |
|---|---|---|
| One extra compose file used with the real one | Only what differs is written; the two can't drift | A separate demo compose file (two to keep in step) |
| Postgres on the server, not a hosted database | Costs nothing more; the data is made up and rebuilt nightly | The host's managed Postgres (more money for made-up data) |
| The reset only drops a database it made (a tag) and only as the superuser | Its last line of defence can't be fooled by a login that sees no firms | Trusting the firm check alone (the safety review showed how that could pass) |
| A database rule refusing real people | Covers every way in at once; the app can't remove it | Checks in each form and import (many places to keep in step) |
| Gate B left as it is, parked | Changing it breaks the browser tests' imports; it only matters for a future real server with a local database | Changing it now (PLAN.md Parking lot, "Connect hosting") |

### What's parked or needs Dylan

- **Merge pull request #14**, then follow `docs/runbooks.md` "A demo server" on your Hetzner server.
  You'll need: the server's IP address, access to your domain's DNS, a GitHub token
  (`read:packages`), and a password you choose for the site.
- Parked: Gate B must learn the difference between a development database and a real server's
  local one, before any real server runs Postgres locally (PLAN.md, "Connect hosting").

## 2026-10-20 · Phase 17: easier to scan (17.1 the look and the casual app, 17.2 the office)

**Time:** 3.1 hours (2.6, then 0.5 for the fourth audit round). **Result:**
`VERIFIED tree=f41c7029251632aa e2e=true` (5,101 unit and integration tests, 93 browser tests and
the 6-step walkthrough, all passing). The `claims-auditor` said **DONE for 17.2** (the office) and,
in a fourth round you allowed, **DONE for 17.1** (the look and the casual app). Both are ticked.
Committed and pushed on `claude/wizardly-faraday-8lg4g0`, pull request #17.

You asked for the app to be "easy for both office and casuals to scan and see where they are
looking to go", with less showing at once and better graphics and colours. Nothing the app does
changed: every button, rule and log entry is still there. What changed is how each main screen
is laid out and how it looks.

### How it was done

- **The UI skills you chose.** Anthropic's `frontend-design`, `design-taste-frontend` (which now
  includes `redesign-existing-projects`) and `ui-ux-pro-max`, read through before use and installed
  for this session only (not into the project).
- **Measured first.** The design skill's inspector (a tool that opens a page in a real browser and
  measures it) looked at the screens as they were. The office dashboard showed only 7 of its 17
  sections on the first screen, and every page title was the size of a website's headline.
- **A style chosen for this product.** The skill's "accent ladder": the firm's own colour used
  only on a few small things (its main buttons, the current tab), pale tints of it for areas, and
  everything else calm and neutral. Each firm sees its own colour, which is what a branded portal
  promises.

### What changed, in plain words

**Everywhere:**
- Flat: no shadows (soft grey glows under boxes) except on the two menus that float over the page,
  thin lines, smaller page titles.
- No small firm name above every page title (the coloured bar at the top already names the firm).
  Three office pages that used that spot as a link back to their list ("Cancellations",
  "Enquiries") now have a proper "‹ back" link instead.
- The firm's own colour (Acme's blue, Northline's teal) on the casual app's main buttons, its
  current tab and the office's current menu item. Only a checked colour code (`#rrggbb`) can ever
  reach the page; without one, the app's own dark ink stands.
- Red, amber and green only where something needs someone: an urgent tag is a pale tint now, not
  a solid red block.

**Casual app:**
- **Home:** the next shift reads top to bottom: when (big), what, where, then the details as a
  short list. "Open in maps" and "Ask for a change" sit side by side; "Cancel this shift" is quiet
  red words (it still opens the same confirmation). Urgent and open shifts are rows in one card.
- **Open shifts:** lighter cards; no "Back to my shifts" button (the tab bar does that).
- **Pay:** the newest week first and big, every line of it (payroll can send a line per rate),
  then earlier weeks as tidy rows.

**Office:**
- **Dashboard:** the four counts are one strip (the first number red only when there are urgent
  shifts). What needs someone comes first: urgent shifts and "Needs attention" side by side with
  the latest cancellations and enquiries. What casuals can see is a compact table at the bottom,
  one row per event with its switch.
- **Events & sessions:** each event's full sessions fold away under "18 filled sessions" (and so
  on); the ones still needing people show first. The page went from 4,631 to 2,971 pixels tall.
- **Casuals:** a coloured tag only for a problem (an expired licence stands out in red); valid
  licences and up-to-date photos are quiet grey words; a missing photo is a small amber dot.

### Decisions made

| Decision | Why | What else was considered |
|---|---|---|
| Keep every action, move nothing off Home | Your tests and walkthrough rely on them, and casuals need them on the day | Moving "Ask for a change" and "Cancel" to the shift's page (fewer buttons on Home, but an extra tap on the day) |
| The firm's own colour on main buttons only | Each firm sees its own brand, while the screens stay calm | Colour on every button and heading (louder), or no brand colour at all (generic) |
| Fold filled sessions on Events, don't hide them | Everything stays one tap away, including each session's menu | Showing only open sessions (a full one would need its own page to reach) |
| A pale firm colour isn't given an outline | The words on the button always read (4.58:1 or more), which is what identifies a button | Adding an outline when the colour is close to the page (parked, revisit with a pilot firm) |

### Found and fixed along the way

- **The safety review (guardrails) found a real pay mistake** before it reached anyone: a week
  that payroll sends with two pay rates would have shown one of its lines under "Earlier weeks".
  Fixed (every line of the newest week shows together), with a test in the order the app really
  loads pay lines, which would have failed before the fix. Your demo's made-up pay has one rate per
  week, so it never showed there.
- 12 smaller points from the same review: the Events fold now uses the app's own "is it full?"
  check (a session the office marked filled folds too); the final "Yes, remove it" for a travel
  area is solid red again; the current tab's icon is filled in, so it doesn't rely on colour;
  "Gross pay" isn't read out twice to screen readers; automatic events don't repeat their name in
  the dashboard table; and the docs describe what was built.
- **The browser tests caught a vanishing confirmation** on an enquiry's page. After the office
  answered a message, the page refreshed itself, and since the message was no longer waiting the
  page dropped the whole "Answer it" section, taking "Closed. No reply was sent." with it. Now the
  form draws that section itself and keeps it, with its confirmation, after the refresh. A new test
  pins both cases (waiting: the forms; answered and loaded fresh: no section).
- **The independent auditor's first round said "not done" on both tasks**, for honest reasons:
  three boxes still had a soft shadow, the office's Menu button still had a thick border, and two
  rules had no test holding them. Fixed: the shadows are gone (only the two menus that float over
  the page keep one), the Menu button is thin like every office control, and two new tests hold the
  rules in place (below). Its second round passed the office task and asked for tests on the rest
  of the look (the 24px title, Home's buttons and rows, pale tags, the current tab's tint), which
  were added. It also spotted that the Booked screen's next-shift card still had a thick black
  frame: it is now a thin-line card like Home's, and its Upcoming / Past switch matches the
  Open | Booked one above it.

### What the new tests prove

- Pay lines are grouped by week, in the loader's order (the bug above).
- The firm's colour reaches the page only as a checked `#rrggbb`, only for a firm with its own
  colour, and never when the brand couldn't be read.
- In a real browser, Acme's "Send me a link" button is Acme's blue and Northline's is teal.
- The dashboard's new order, its one-strip counts (red only with urgent shifts) and its table.
- The Events fold: open sessions listed, full and marked-filled ones folded, every session keeping
  its menu, and "1 filled session" (not "sessions").
- The page header's back link.
- The Casuals list's rule: a valid licence and an up-to-date photo are plain grey words with no
  tag; a missing photo is the small dot and "Missing"; expiring, expired, due soon, overdue and
  waiting keep their coloured tag.
- Pay shows no "Back to my shifts" anywhere; the two Shifts-tab switches share one style; every
  Open shifts card is a thin-line card with no red edge, the urgent one tagged in a pale tint.
- Flat everywhere: a scan of all the app's code finds a shadow only on the two floating menus, and
  every office button and field (and the Menu button) has a 1px border. Adding a shadow anywhere
  else, or a thick border to an office control, fails this test.
- The enquiry page's "Answer it" section stays with its confirmation after the page refreshes.
- The rest of the look: every page title 24px with no line under it; tags are pale tints for every
  status (urgent too), never a solid fill; the current tab sits on the firm colour's pale tint;
  Home's "Open in maps" and "Ask for a change" sit side by side with "Cancel this shift" as quiet
  red words under them; urgent and open shifts are rows in one card; every card has one thin line.
- Dark text stays readable on the firm colour's pale tint whatever colour a firm picks: checked
  against 30 colours (the default, both demo firms and 27 spread across all colours) in light and
  dark.

### What's parked or needs Dylan

- **17.1 needed a fourth audit round, which you allowed.** The rules (`CLAUDE.md` rule 13) allow
  three, then stop and report. After the third, the app passed every check but the auditor wanted
  three more tests. They were added: Pay has no "Back to my shifts" button; both Shifts-tab
  switches use one shared style (`switchTrack`; it had been written out twice); every Open shifts
  card is a thin-line card with no red edge, urgent or not. The fourth round said DONE.
- The auditor's last notes, not blocking: the error and "not found" pages keep their larger
  titles (the 24px rule is for app screens); no test checks the order of Home's shift facts after
  the address; no database test loads a week paid at two rates through the pay loader (the screen
  test feeds lines in the loader's real order).
- **#16 was merged at its work-in-progress commit.** This pull request brings the checked version
  (the pay fix, the enquiry fix and the review's fixes) onto `main`.
- Parked (PLAN.md Parking lot): the dashboard still reads 2 sessions per event it no longer shows;
  a pale firm colour's button edge; the office's other screens and the casual's Profile and shift
  page keep their old layout under the new look.

### How to see it

After merging, your demo server picks it up within a few minutes of the image being built. Open
`https://acme.staffingresourcingtest.uk` on your phone (Home, Shifts, Pay) and
`https://acme.staffingresourcingtest.uk/office` on a laptop (Dashboard, Events & sessions,
Casuals).

## 2026-10-21 · Phase 18: the redesign (18.1 to 18.5)

Time spent: 3.5 hours (see the Hours log in PLAN.md).

### The result

- Verify: `VERIFIED tree=56617d45b91d8c3f e2e=true` (the check script, with the browser tests, on
  the files the auditor read; the ticks and these notes came after it).
- Tests: 2,942 unit tests and 2,215 integration tests pass, none skipped; 94 browser tests and
  the boss walkthrough's 6 pass.
- Auditor (the independent checker that reads only the code, tests and logs): three rounds.
  Round 1 stopped at the gate, because the work had been committed after the check script ran, so
  the stamp no longer matched the files (my mistake; the fix was to check again and commit only
  after the audit). Round 2: 18.1 DONE, 18.2 to 18.5 not yet (gaps listed below). Round 3: all
  four DONE, "the phase's Done when line is met".
- Commits: `5dbef09` (the build) and the one carrying this entry (round 2's fixes and the
  ticks), pushed to `claude/wizardly-faraday-8lg4g0` (pull request #18).

### What was built, in plain words

Dylan asked for the Claude Design boards to be built into the app, with the fonts and colours
reworked using the design skills. The boards were the approved mock-up (the "before and after"
picture was shown first, as the project's rules ask).

- **The look (18.1).** Two new fonts, both stored inside the app (no font service is called):
  Public Sans for words, and Barlow Condensed, a narrow font drawn from UK road signs, for times,
  dates, gates and counts. Colours follow safety signs: red means act now, sign yellow means
  something needs doing, green means done. Each firm's own colour is worked out twice, once for
  light and once for dark, so text on it is always readable (contrast at least 4.5 to 1, the
  accessibility standard). The top of every casual page is now white, with the firm's initial on
  its colour. Shared parts: a **date tile** (the day of the week over a big date), a **sign
  plate** (a gate or post on a yellow plate), **seats** (one small square per place, filled when
  taken). Animations are short (150 to 250 thousandths of a second) and turn into a simple fade
  for anyone whose phone asks for less motion.
- **Casual Home (18.2).** The next shift is a **pass**, like a lanyard card: day, time, role,
  place and gate, which opens in place to show getting there, where to report, what to bring and
  wear, pay, and the buttons. "Can't make it?" opens a **sheet** (a panel that rises from the
  bottom) with the strike warning, the reason, "Cancel this shift", "Ask for a change instead" and
  "Keep my shift". Under it, **For you**: at most four rows (urgent shifts, what needs doing, her
  home area if she hasn't set one, shifts to apply for).
- **Open shifts and taking one (18.3).** The next 8 days as a strip at the top (booked days filled
  in, urgent days red). Urgent shifts first, then the rest, one card each. A card opens the
  shift's page, which looks like the pass, with "Take shift" in a bar that stays at the bottom of
  the screen.
- **The office (18.4).** The menu in groups: Today, Pay, People, and "Admin and records" folded
  away. The dashboard now answers one question, "what needs me now?": a heading like "3 things
  need you", this week as seven days, then **Next up** (one urgent place at a time, with its
  buttons), **After that** (one row per kind of to-do), **Waiting on others** and **Sorted
  today**. The full to-do list moved to its own page, **Needs attention**, which the bell opens.
- **Events & sessions (18.5).** A two-week **rota board**: one row per event, each session a small
  "3/4" pill on its day (red for an urgent gap). A row opens in place into its sessions' table.

### Things found and fixed along the way

| What | Why it mattered | What was done |
|---|---|---|
| If the urgent list failed to load, the dashboard said "Every urgent place is filled", and with Needs attention failing too, "Nothing needs you right now". | Both would be false: the office could miss an urgent gap. | A part that fails now says so where it shows, and the heading says "At least 2 things need you" or "Some of the dashboard couldn't load". A test holds this. |
| The open-shift cards and the closed pass showed only the drive time ("15 min by car"). | Many stewards don't drive; a car time alone misleads (rule 12G.3 says show both ways). | Cards show the full travel line (miles, car and public transport); the closed pass shows none, and the open pass shows all three. |
| The folded "Admin and records" menu group was a `<details>` element. | Browsers treat it as a "group", and the browser tests find form lines by that role, so they could pick the menu by mistake. | It's now a button that shows or hides the links. |
| A shift held for people with fewer hours lost its "Check again" button. | The Help page tells casuals to tap it. | "Check again" is back on the shift's page. |
| Home's no-signal note said only the time ("from 09:05"). | The saved copy can be opened days later. | It now says the day too ("from 09:05 on Mon 2 Nov"). |
| The office guide and the casual Help page described the old screens. | Help must match the app. | Both rewritten for the new screens. |
| The "Can't make it?" sheet could be closed while a cancel was being sent (the safety review's one must-fix). | She could leave without seeing whether she had cancelled, and with a strike. | While it sends, Escape and tapping outside do nothing and the other two buttons are greyed out; if it is closed anyway, it opens again with the answer. A browser test holds the cancel back on purpose and checks this. |
| The dashboard's week counted sessions, not places. | A session with 4 places and 1 taken looked the same as one with 1 place. | It counts places: "3 open", "2 urgent", "All filled", and once a day's sessions have started, how many were filled ("4 of 6 filled"). |
| An event's open row on the rota board listed every session, however many. | An event with 30 sessions made one very long table. | It lists the two weeks on the board and says how many more are to come; the event's own page has them all. The "…" menu column stays in view when the table is wider than the screen. |
| Earlier and Later used whatever date was in the address. | A made-up date could break the board. | Only a real date is accepted; anything else shows this fortnight. |

### Decisions

| Decision | Why | What else was considered |
|---|---|---|
| What a shift needs (licences) is on its page, not on the list card. | The design shows it there, to show less at once; every card is a shift she can take anyway. | Keeping the tags on every card (more clutter, which Dylan asked to cut). |
| The Home list of everything that needs updating became one row (the worst thing, then "and 2 more", to Profile). | The design's For you row; Profile lists every qualification with its upload link. | Keeping the full list on Home. |
| The dashboard's heading is now the headline ("3 things need you"); the browser tab still says "Dashboard". | The design. | Keeping "Dashboard" as the heading. |

### What the new tests prove

- The new words (dates, times, "In 3 days", the For you rows, the week strip, the dashboard's
  headline, rows and week, the board's pills): 19 tests in `tests/unit/ui.redesign-copy.test.ts`.
- Every colour pair in both themes passes the contrast test; 30 firm colours in light and dark.
- Only the pass has a shadow; 40px text only in the sign font.
- The dashboard: the headline, each day of the week, one urgent place at a time with its warnings
  (hidden from casuals, offers stopped, the office emailed), every After that row with its count
  and link, a part that couldn't load, and no link repeating a menu link's name. Needs attention's
  page: every item with its link.
- The shared parts and motion: the date tile, the sign plate and the seats draw what they should;
  every animation is 150, 200 or 250 thousandths of a second with one easing; every animation the
  app uses is defined; no animation library is installed; and anyone whose phone asks for less
  motion gets the simple fade (`tests/unit/ui.parts-motion.test.ts`).
- The menu's counts are yellow only when they need someone (pay queries only for payroll), and
  the board's Earlier and Later reach the right fortnight from any real date.
- The browser tests walk each flow on the new screens: logging in, taking a shift from its page,
  cancelling through the sheet (with and without a strike), the urgent board, travel areas, no
  signal, the office menu and its folded group, the dashboard, Needs attention, the rota board's
  showing switch, and the boss walkthrough.

### What's parked or needs Dylan

- Parked: the dashboard still loads three lists it no longer shows (only their counts); the old
  four counts' words are unused. To remove with their tests.
- Parked: the walkthrough page (`walkthrough-site/`) still shows the old screens.
- Parked: some older rows in `docs/screens.md` still describe the screens before the redesign
  (its Phase 18 section says it wins where they differ). To rewrite.
- The auditor's notes, not blocking: "someone else took it" and "offered first to people with
  fewer hours" are checked by unit and database tests, not in a browser; more than two sessions of
  one event on one day share one pill with "×3" (every session is still in the open row's table).
- Two changes the design made, to confirm: what a shift needs (licences) is on its page, not on
  the list cards; Home's list of everything that needs updating is one "Needs doing" row leading
  to Profile.
- Check on your phone: the pass, the sheet, the strip and the board, in light and dark.

### How to see it

Nothing is on the demo server yet: pull request #18 is a draft. Once it's merged, the demo server
picks it up within a few minutes of the image being built. Then open
`https://acme.staffingresourcingtest.uk` on your phone (Home, Shifts) and

sessions).

## 2026-10-21 · Phase 19: the strike's answer stays on screen (19.1)

Time spent: 1.2 hours (see the Hours log in PLAN.md).

### The result

- Verify: `VERIFIED tree=bace7884f6b1e3b6 e2e=true` (the check script, with the browser tests, on
  the files the auditor read; the tick and these notes came after it).
- Tests: 5,159 unit and integration tests pass, none skipped; 96 browser tests and the boss
  walkthrough's 6 pass.
- Auditor: round 1 NOT DONE: the code did what the task says, but three behaviours had no test
  (which buttons a deleted casual's or a withdrawn strike's record shows; "Kept, …" outliving the
  refresh on a deleted casual's strike; the withdraw form closing once the strike can't be
  withdrawn). Round 2, after those tests: DONE, every claim proven, no fakes.

### What was wrong, in plain words

On a cancellation's record, "Withdraw strike…" answers "Withdrawn. It no longer counts, and the
casual sees it as withdrawn." and then refreshes the record. The page only drew the strike's
buttons while the strike was live, and the answer lived inside the buttons' box, so the refresh
removed the box and the answer with it. Whether anyone saw the answer depended on how fast the
refresh came back, which is why `tests/e2e/office.strikes.spec.ts` failed now and then (it caused
the NOT VERIFIED on `36b7468`). "Keep strike" on a deleted casual's strike had the same problem.

### What changed

- `src/app/office/cancellations/[ref]/page.tsx`: the strike's buttons are always rendered, in the
  same place, for whoever may change strikes, so React keeps their answer across the refresh (the
  way "Resend evidence" already worked, 12E.1).
- `src/app/office/cancellations/[ref]/strike-actions.tsx`: shows nothing when there's no button and
  no answer; the withdraw form shows only while the strike can still be withdrawn.
- `src/lib/office/strike-actions-copy.ts`: `strikeButtons()`, the one rule for which buttons show
  ("Withdraw strike…" while live and the casual's details aren't deleted; "Keep strike" while live
  and not reviewed).
- Tests: `tests/unit/strikes.page.test.ts` (the rule for every case; the buttons both, one or none,
  and nothing at all); `tests/e2e/office.strikes.spec.ts` (the answer still there once the heading
  reads "Strike: Issued, then withdrawn"; a deleted casual's strike kept; a strike withdrawn
  elsewhere while the form was open); `tests/e2e/db.ts` (`eraseCasual`, `withdrawStrikeAs`, both
  through the real code).
- `docs/screens.md`: the answer stays on screen after the record refreshes.

### What the tests prove (each seen to fail with its bug put back)

- Before the fix, the strengthened browser test failed every time, at "the answer is still there".
- The old page rule put back: all three strike browser tests fail at the answer check.
- The form's close rule taken out: the new form test fails at "the form has closed".
- "Keep strike" allowed on a withdrawn strike: the rule's unit test fails.

### Setup notes for a fresh container (none of this changes the repo)

- Playwright 1.63 asks for browser build 1243; `/root/pw-compat` links it to the installed 1194
  (`PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH=/root/pw-compat`).
- The CI-mode browser run is a production server, which refuses `.env.example`'s
  `SESSION_SECRET`: give the local `.env` a random one.
- `dockerd` started by hand; `caddy:2.10` from `mirror.gcr.io` (Docker Hub answered 429).

### What needs Dylan

- Nothing for this task. Separately, the reference-based redesign mock-up ("ShiftDesk Pass
  Redesign") waits for his answers: build it or not, the dark theme, the Pay day tile, one count
  of 10 for the office, and moving the board's show/hide switch.

### Follow-up: the browser test on GitHub (2026-10-21)

- **What failed.** PR #19's `e2e` check failed in `office.strikes.spec.ts`: the second and third
  tests logged in as the same office user as the first. Their login link was looked for from one
  second before "Send me a link" (the spec's grace for clock drift), so the first test's link,
  already used, could be picked up ("This link has expired or was already used."); Playwright's
  retry then asked for a fourth link to that address inside 15 minutes, which the login limit
  refuses (3 per address: "Too many tries. Try again in 14 minutes.").
- **Fix.** Each test logs in as its own made-up office user (`strikes.resourcer`,
  `strikes.reviewer`, `strikes.second`); nothing in the app changed.
- **Proof.** With `--repeat-each=2` the old spec failed its second round with "Too many tries";
  the fixed spec passed 6 of 6.
- **Result.** `RUN_E2E=1 ./scripts/verify.sh`: VERIFIED tree=1a56724f8c7be140 e2e=true.

## 2026-10-22 · Phase 21: the office's look (21.1 to 21.4)

Time spent: 4.5 hours (from Dylan's brief at 10:22 to the last tick, measured from the session
history; see the Hours log in PLAN.md).

### The result

- Verify: `VERIFIED tree=0fab60e990c5be25 e2e=true` on the files the round-3 auditors read
  (`VERIFIED tree=08c5ff104e032ed4 e2e=true` for round 2, `ab6a1f2da3806f82` for round 1). The
  ticks and these notes came after.

  walkthrough's 6 pass, none flaky.
- Auditor: round 1, all four NOT DONE (missing proof more than missing code, but also real gaps:
  40px menu rows, white menu counts, shared parts off the system, no error state on Waiting on
  others). Round 2: 21.1 and 21.3 DONE; 21.2 (two tests) and 21.4 (the phone's skeleton behind the
  drawer, thin page checks) NOT DONE. Round 3: 21.2 and 21.4 DONE. No fakes found in any round.

### What changed, in plain words

- **The office's own look, from one set of colours (light and dark):** a warm off-white page,
  white cards with one soft shadow, near-black main buttons, grey pill buttons, red words on grey
  for a destructive step, and red, amber, blue and green only for statuses and the dashboard's
  tinted cards. Geist for words, Instrument Serif for each page's headline, Geist Mono for the big
  numbers (tried beside heavy Geist; the picture is in `docs/mockups/phase-21`). Seven text sizes,
  seven spacing steps, two corner sizes and pills, 150 to 200ms transitions; a test checks every
  office file, and every shared part an office page shows, keeps to them.
- **The dashboard:** the date line over a serif greeting; four tinted cards that jump to their
  sections; the urgent places as one rounded list with "3 of 4 filled" bars, "Not offered yet" in
  red and a small black "Offer"; this week as bars Monday to Sunday with a dashed "All filled" line;
  tinted panels of near-white rows. Every section has its loading skeleton, empty words and error
  words (Waiting on others too, which used to take the whole page down if its count failed).
- **The menu:** on the page's ground with a grey pill for the page you're on, 44px rows (the
  drawer's too), one grey count pill, the week and pay day on two lines at its foot.
- **Every other page** on the same parts, at 390px and 1440px in both themes. A long qualification
  no longer runs Casuals sideways on a phone; a cancellation's record is one white card.
- **The dark round button over the menu's foot** was Next's development button (only `next dev`
  draws it, bottom-left by default): it's bottom-right now.
- **Loading skeletons** come from the menu's own links. A route-wide loading screen was tried and
  removed: it made pages a role can't open answer "200 OK" instead of "not found" (the browser
  tests caught it). On a phone the drawer now shuts as you pick, so the skeleton is seen.
- **The casual app is unchanged.** Its counts, cards and buttons kept Phase 20's look (its Booked
  count had changed by accident; it's back).
- **Outside the brief:** a test file could hand out the shared "number no firm has", so the voice
  test failed now and then; it no longer can.

"3 of 4 filled" in place of "1 of 4 places free"; "Offer" (still named "Offer to someone" for
screen readers); "Pay Fri 30 Oct" in place of "Pay on Fri 30 Oct"; "No sessions" on an empty day;
the four cards jump to their sections (each section keeps its link to the whole list).

Time spent: 4.5 hours (from Dylan's round-2 message at 19:28 to the last audit; 0.5 for the
mock-up and taking the gradients out, 4.0 to build, verify and audit; see the Hours log in
PLAN.md).

### The result

- Verify: `VERIFIED tree=14645a7e44d417b6 e2e=true` on the files the round-3 auditors read
  (`b988286c17c7e423` for round 2, `d0665d32cd8dc58a` for round 1). The ticks and these notes came
  after.
- Tests: 5,226 unit and integration tests pass, none skipped; 108 browser tests and the
  walkthrough's 6 pass, none flaky.
- Auditor: round 1, all four NOT DONE (no fakes; behaviours built but not proven in a browser,
  and parts missed: Needs attention's page, fields and confirmations still Phase 21's, `btn.link`
  not dipping, the every-page look test at 1440px). Round 2: 22.2 and 22.3 DONE, ticked; 22.1
  (controls that didn't dip; no lit top edge in light) and 22.4 (the Team page's steps; pages the
  look test skipped) NOT DONE. Round 3: 22.1 DONE, ticked; 22.4 NOT DONE. Three rounds is the
  limit, so 22.4 stays unticked (below).

### What changed, in plain words

- **A mock-up first, then the build.** Dylan on Phase 21 live: "the use of colours it just looks
  very bland theres still no depth to any of the individual sections and a tacky scroll wheel on
  the left side you've missed out on shaders layers and depth this whole time i also asked for
  animations still none not ai slop just subtle reactive on click responses". The round-2 mock-up
  (`docs/mockups/phase-22/mockup.html`) was approved with one change: "remove the gradients theres
  no need for that". Then it was built.
- **Layers:** a grey window with the page as a sheet set into it; white cards, lists and tables
  with a hairline, a lit top edge and a small shadow of their own; buttons and rows raised a
  little more; menus floating; bars, the board's filter track and every field set in as grooves.
  A fine grain on the sheet, the panels and the colour cards. No gradients (a test checks the
  styles, and the browser tests check nothing is drawn with one).
- **Colour:** the dashboard's four cards in full flat colour with a soft glow of their own colour;
  their panels (and Needs attention's page) washes of raised white rows. The menu's counts take
  their card's colour. Every listed colour pair passes the contrast test in both themes.
- **Type:** DM Sans for words, DM Mono for the big numbers, Newsreader semibold for each page's
  headline, all self-hosted (Geist removed).
- **Movement, only when you do something:** everything you press dips to 97% and springs back
  (90ms down, 300ms back): buttons, openers, switches, rows, cards, pills, tabs, and text links
  with their own 44px target (a link inside a sentence is words, and doesn't move). A card lifts
  2px under the pointer; the menu's white pill slides to a link the moment you pick it; menus grow
  out of their buttons; a card's jump lands with a ring that fades out; "Offer" shows a spinner
  while the offer page opens; skeletons pulse slowly. With reduced motion nothing dips, lifts or
  slides.
- **The menu:** no scrollbar. Rows are 34px with a mouse (44px for a thumb, always in the drawer),
  so every row of an admin's menu fits a 1280 × 640 window; a longer menu scrolls with a fade at
  the edge that has more and opens with your page in view. The firm and your initials sit above
  it; the light/dark switch and the how-to guide moved into your account menu; the pay week is a
  small line at the end.
- **Second steps and answers:** the step before something destructive ("Yes, revoke it", "Yes,
  turn off access") is one red wash with depth (`confirmStep`), before a change that takes
  nothing away ("Yes, make them an admin") a grey one (`checkStep`); notices and statuses with an
  edge have the same depth; an answer under a button is a white slip raised off its form.
- **Found by the screenshots:** at 1280px Needs attention's "Go to the Dashboard" broke onto two
  lines inside its round button (a column 3 of 12, about 171px inside; it needs about 192px). The
  column is 4 of 12 now, as on the dashboard; a browser test checks no round button on any page
  breaks onto two lines (it fails with the old column put back).
- **Found by verify (from before this phase):** on a shift's page, "Cancelled {name}'s booking
  with {9 days'} notice. They'll be told." vanished a moment after it showed: the refresh that
  moves the booking to "Cancelled" took the booking's row, and the words in it, off the list, so
  the browser test passed only when it looked in that moment. The words are now held over the
  Places list, where the refresh doesn't replace them; the test checks they're still there once
  the list says "Nobody booked yet." (with the old code it fails every time).
- **The how-to guide told the old layout:** it said your name sits "at the bottom of the menu" on
  a wide screen, that "the moon or sun beside it" switches the theme, and listed itself as a menu
  link. It now says your name is at the top of the menu, beside your firm's name, that it also
  switches between dark and light ("Switch to dark mode" or "Switch to light mode"), and lists
  only what the menu shows. `docs/walkthrough.md` had the same old description (with the bell
  that went in Phase 20); fixed too.
- **Tests, each seen to fail with its feature taken out:** the "…" menu's pop, the day tooltip,
  the menu's edge fade, `office:press` on `btn.link`, the board's buttons dipping, the skeleton's
  pulse, the press timings, the lit edge in light, and an edged wash without depth.
- **PLAN.md's 22.2 wording:** its test line said "the guide one tap from every page"; the guide
  is in the account menu (two taps), so it now says "the guide in the account menu on every page",
  which is what was built and tested, and was changed before any audit.
- **The browser tests' firm addresses:** this container lost its hosts line for `acme.localhost`
  and Docker had stopped; both were put back in the container (nothing in the repo changed).

### What needs Dylan

- 22.4 (every other page) is not ticked: after three audit rounds the auditor still found two

  with a test that fails on the old code); a second review of those fixes, each finding checked by
