  browser test checks the login pages at 390px), and the screenshots' tooltip line in the README
  described only the columns' tooltip (now says both).

### What changed, in plain words

- **Two mock-ups first.** Dylan on round 2 live: "i told you to remove the gradients and the
  colours are still wrong what your making is like 2008 apple notes app look it needs to be
  modern". The round 3 mock-up was approved ("looks good now add more graphs and visuals that help
  the office team what needs work on…"), then the same mock-up with six charts and their panels
  ("go build it"). `docs/mockups/phase-23/` has it, with screenshots of the build beside it.
- **The look:** flat. A light grey ground, white cards with a 1px hairline, no shadow at rest; one
  soft shadow only on what floats (menus, the drawer, the toast, a chart's tooltip and its panel).
  Plus Jakarta Sans for every word and number (DM Sans, DM Mono and Newsreader removed). One sky
  blue main button a screen, with near-black words; the other buttons white pills with a grey
  edge. Chips are tinted pills; a new blue "waiting" tone is for things waiting on someone else.
- **The shell:** a white top bar the full width at every width (the firm, the pay week with "This
  week", the shifts line's number, a square light or dark button, your initials and name); the
  menu in its own white card from 1024px, the page in another. An admin's whole menu fits a
  1280 × 640 window.
- **The dashboard:** the four counts are white cards that open their rows in a panel (from the
  side on a laptop, a sheet from the bottom on a phone); the urgent places are cards with a wide
  "Offer"; six charts replace the week strip and the three text panels: places to fill over the
  next 2 weeks, which events need people, this week's offers, how long things have waited,
  cancellations over the last 2 weeks, and licences running out in the next 8 weeks. Every bar,
  dot and count opens the real rows with their actions; each chart has a sentence written from its
  data, a legend, its figures on hover or keyboard focus, and a Numbers table. Every link the old
  panels had is still on the page.
- **Every other office page** on round 3's parts: the "Show" filters are tabs, notices and code
  boxes have their own parts, today on the events board is a sky circle, and the how-to guide
  describes the new layout.
- **The demo's made-up data** gains this week's offers: 10 waiting for a reply and up to 12 "said
  yes" (the seed writes only what the app itself can reach: no "said no", which the app can't
  record yet; none in Acme's quiet hours; the offer-count lock taken like every offer writer).
- **Found by the reviews (each fixed, with a test):** the seed made up "said no" offers; the
  licences chart's "booked after it runs out" came from a list that stops at 50; a panel could say
  "Showing the first 0."; sentences that were false at the edges ("Every place … is filled" while
  a started session was short; a hidden-places count cut at 50 rows; "Needs attention has them
  all", when its lists stop at 50 too); the tooltip couldn't be hovered or dismissed with Escape
  (WCAG 1.4.13), and once hoverable it covered the bar above (now beside a row of bars, and a click
  on it goes on to what's under it); the sticky top bar could hide a focused control (the office
  now keeps 80px clear at the top); a licence name could push the page sideways; on a phone the
  cancellations chart jumped back to today when the toolbar moved.
- **Found by verify:** the first two full runs used `next dev` for the browser tests. Late in each
  run the dev server's memory jumped from about 2 GB to 10 to 12 GB within a minute, while it
  compiled two voice webhook routes for the first time (unchanged code but for one date helper).
  The container's 14.3 GB cap killed it the first time (a WhatsApp test failed), and a test timed
  out the second. A Turbopack memory setting didn't stop it, so it was taken out again. The third
  run used a production build, as CI does (the server peaked at 396 MB): all green. The details
  are in the Parking lot.

### What needs Dylan

- **The office's `brand` colour in light is the sky's darker shade (#0284c7), not the sky.** The
  sky on white is 2.77:1, and a control's edge needs 3:1. Nothing in the office paints with it now
  (the events board's today and the login link's button take the sky itself); it is kept for any
  shared part that might. In dark it is the sky.
- **The demo's offers chart** shows "said yes" only from about 07:20 on Thursday (the seed's

  down (the Parking lot's "No thanks" item).
- **22.4 stays unticked**, as decided: round 3 replaced round 2's look before its fourth audit.
- Small things parked, all older than this phase or minor: the guide's "View sessions" line and
  its missing Enquiries line, the erase form's red strip, the Strikes summary under 44px, the
  licence dots' figures covering a close dot above, and the browser tests' dev server memory.

## 2026-10-30 · Phase 25.2 (P3's screens): the firm's model on the casual's and the office's screens

### Dylan's approvals

- **The casual's mock-up** (https://claude.ai/artifact/Wf4qCNqTTDq5Ytq3TryAJw), answered in the
  chat on 2026-10-30: "Yes, build as shown"; a fortnight's pay row gives its dates; with strikes
  off, Help leaves its strikes section out. Built in commit 3a20c65.
- **The office's mock-up** (https://claude.ai/artifact/Nu2R4jTRb4qmrhPXxkGg9d, real screenshots
  before and after at 390px and 1280px at 150%), answered in the chat on 2026-10-30: "Yes, build
  as shown" (with the table for the sheet's names as proposed); a fortnight in the firm's own
  word ("Cycle 22"); "Who asked for it?" required; "None: strikes are off" kept.

### What changed in the office, in plain words

- **The pay cycle.** The dashboard's head, the pay dashboard (its header, "Sorted this cycle", the
  pay day with nothing imported, "Recently sorted") and payroll's pay-query headings and digest
  email follow the firm's pay calendar, in its own word: "Cycle 22 · Pay Fri 13 Nov", "the cycle
  of Mon 12 Oct". The AI's summary line is told it's a cycle, so it can't call a fortnight a week.
  A weekly firm reads as before. The track's "This week" stays Monday to Sunday.
- **Who asked.** Both of the office's cancel forms ask "Who asked for it?" (the firm, the client,
  the casual by name for one booking, or not known), required. Only the office's answer is used:
  a casual's own cancel is always hers, and the planner's stays not known. The notice report and
  its CSV show it in an "Asked by" column.
- **The name map.** The shift import says how many rows the name map left out; the action log says
  what each change to the map did ("The name map now skips the role "holiday"."); and Needs
  attention lists, under "Names your rota sheet uses", each role name the sheet uses that the app
  can't read and each skipped name on a session still to come. These come from a new table,
  `sheet_name_problems` (migration 0061: firm_id, forced RLS, a TABLE_ACCESS entry, isolation
  fixtures and both composite foreign keys tested), which each sync replaces. It keeps a role
  cell's cleaned text only, never a name, mobile or staff id, and never in the action log.
- **Either licence.** The office's event cards, session rows and cancellation records show a group
  as "SIA SG or SIA DS"; a session that asks for one outright shows each as before.

### Checks

- New and changed tests: `tests/unit/office.firm-model.test.ts`,
  `tests/unit/office.cancel-asked-by.test.ts`, the Needs attention and board unit tests, the
  parser's, and the integration tests for who asked (`cancel.asked-by`, `roster.api-caller`), the
  notice report, the sheet's names (`roster.names`), the pay dashboard, the board, events and
  cancellations; three browser tests now answer "Who asked for it?", and one checks the report.
- The `guardrails-reviewer` found no golden rule broken and four smaller points, all fixed with
  tests:
  - a role cell's text could hold contact details typed by mistake: text with an email address or
    7 or more digits is never kept, and switching the sheet off or replacing it deletes its rows;
  - past the 200-row and 50-item caps, the skipped names (the sessions to act on) could be pushed
    out by unreadable ones: they're kept first, and still shown after them;
  - an unreachable fallback to the raw cell is gone (it could have failed the table's check);
  - the AI's line may use only the firm's own word for its period ("week" or "cycle").
- Mutation checks: 51 changes made on purpose to the new code, each now caught by a test. Four
  were missed at first: a staff row's first unreadable role, the list's order, the office's own
  report rows' "Asked by" and the cancellation record's "A or B". Tests were added for each, and
  each was then caught.

### Left for later (Parking lot)

- Who asked on a cancellation's own record and the Cancellations CSV.
- Cancelling a skipped name's session straight from Needs attention.

## 2026-10-30 · Phase 25.3 (P4's office half): the office for a big firm

### Dylan's approvals

- The mock-up (https://claude.ai/artifact/RvpGDfQMcTAqtfCTYJFpUk): build as shown; the offer limit
  fixed at 25 for each free place; the combined email once, at the end of the hour; a held pay
  question in Enquiries' Pay view.
- Two of the three action-log indexes in the mock-up couldn't work: under the firm walls (RLS)
  Postgres won't use an index on `data ->> 'workerId'`, because jsonb's functions aren't
  leakproof. Measured on a made-up firm (2,000 casuals, 700,000 log entries), the Worker filter
  took 0.6 to 0.85 s and the Shift filter 7 to 154 s. Dylan approved two generated columns instead,
  `worker_ref` and `shift_ref` (copied from each entry's data, no new information), each with an
  index: 1.5 to 4 ms.
- "just do what you would recommend stop prompting me and build it": from here each mock-up is
  still published, then built as recommended without waiting for an answer.

### What changed in the office, in plain words

- **Why a message got no reply.** With automatic replies off, an enquiry says what the message was
  sorted as and that replies are off ("A keyword matched: "pay". Automatic replies are off, so it
  was passed to a person."), not "They asked for a person". A held pay question is in the Pay
  view. The action log says the same.
- **Offers stop after 25 for each free place.** An urgent session's waves stop once 25 casuals for
  each place still free have been offered it; the dashboard's card says so in red, and the log
  says how many. Another place coming free raises the limit.
- **One email instead of a flood.** Past the 5 unknown-number emails in an hour, the rest go in one
  email when the hour ends, to each admin and resourcer, once.
- **Real counts.** Needs attention's lists still show their first 50, but the heading and the
  menu's count say how many there really are ("Showing the first 50 of 400.").
- **Faster, not visible.** Every office page's counts are read in one transaction, and Needs
  attention's page uses that reading instead of working it out again. The webhook's status is
  read from a small row per firm (`webhook_status`, migration 0062) instead of searching the log.

### Checks

- New tests: `tests/unit/office.big-firm.test.ts` (every new line against `docs/screens.md`),
  `tests/unit/office.frame-page.test.ts`, `tests/integration/office.frame.test.ts` (one
  transaction, each role's counts, isolation), the offer limit in `offers.waves.test.ts`, the
  combined email in `inbound.whatsapp.test.ts`, the webhook status row in `webhooks.test.ts`, the
  worker-list entries and the indexes' use under RLS in `office.action-log.test.ts`, and a list
  past 50 in the Needs attention unit test; the new table is in the isolation fixtures.
- The `guardrails-reviewer` found no golden rule broken (no critical, high or medium findings) and
  five low ones. Four are fixed with tests:
  - a wave run again at the limit after dying before it queued the next could end the chain with
    no stop logged: it queues the next wave (once), which logs it;
  - the action log finds a worker named in an entry's list of workers only under two actions: the
    log now refuses such a list under any other action (`src/lib/events/worker-lists.ts`);
  - a test's made-up phone numbers moved into Ofcom's drama range;
  - the at-most-once cases of the combined email, and that migration 0062 rewrites the action log
    under a lock at deploy, are written down (`docs/api-and-jobs.md`, `docs/runbooks.md`).

## 2026-10-30 · Phase 25.5 (P7.4 and P7.5): the staffing inbox in Enquiries

### Dylan's go-ahead

- "just do what you would recommend stop prompting me and build it": the mock-up was published
  for the record (https://claude.ai/artifact/VAnarrjGdN81yJnuydWa8d) and built as recommended.

### What changed, in plain words

- Emails to a firm's staffing inbox are sorted as the shifts line sorts a message (a keyword,
  else the AI's label, else a person) and shown on Enquiries beside the messages: an envelope, the
  subject on its own line, their words. Mail that isn't from one of the firm's casuals is left for
  the firm's own inbox, where it already is: listed as "Left for your inbox", never in Need a
  person, its words never kept.
- With the firm's automatic replies on, a casual asking for shifts, to cancel or about pay gets the
  link by email at once, to the address the firm holds for them (never the one the email came
  from), with the firm's inbox as its Reply-To so their answer comes back to Enquiries; at most 3 a
  casual a day. With them off, it waits for a person.
- An email for a person waits in Need a person with the AI's draft of a reply (when the AI is
  connected): from that email alone, marked as the AI's, never for pay or money. The office checks
  it, changes it and sends it by email, or closes it with a reason. A new AI use, added to its
  tripwire test on purpose.
- An email stored more than 3 hours before it could be sorted goes to a person unsorted (a stale
  "cancel me" isn't one); an hourly sweep queues any whose job was lost.
- The office gets the inbox's summary every 3 hours of the firm's day when emails came in:
  counts only.
- One new table (`email_replies`: what went back, as sent; deleted with its email) and the AI's
  draft on the email. The inbox's signing secret is no longer refused on a production server, now
  that its emails are answered; the provider and the privacy notice's line still come first
  (Parking lot).
- Words in `docs/screens.md` ("The staffing inbox in Enquiries") and `docs/messaging-templates.md`
  ("The staffing inbox's emails", `inbox_summary`) first.

### Checks

- Tests: the sorting job on the real database (each path, handled once, the 3-a-day limit, stale
  and former casuals, the last try, the sweep, an erasure mid-draft), the office's reply by email
  and close (from the draft as it was, changed, or their own words; refused cases; another firm's
  never found), Enquiries with emails, the summary (once a slot, its counts, a failed and a
  part-way link email), the export, erasure and purge taking the replies; every new line checked
  against the docs; the AI tripwire extended on purpose; a browser test answering an email.
- The `guardrails-reviewer`: no critical or high finding. Fixed: a pay question could still get a
  draft (the money words widened, the draft's own words screened too); the summary counted a
  failed link email as "answered by your office" (a failed link email now goes back to Need a
  person, its root cause); the summary's slot is the email's arrival; the log says whether the
  draft went as it was, was changed, or wasn't used, and `email.drafted` records each draft; no
  draft without an address on file; no names in a draft; the runbook's stale lines; a log key the
  masker hid. Not done: tying a reply's casual to its email's in the database (both inserts copy
  the email's own casual, so no caller can mismatch them), and the database check for a held pay
  email (Parking lot).
- Mutation checks: 53 changes made on purpose to the new code, each caught by a test. Three were
  missed at first (a pay label with no money word, an erasure mid-draft, the office's job given an
  automatic reply); tests were added, and each was then caught.
- `RUN_E2E=1 ./scripts/verify.sh` printed VERIFIED (`tree=16bbc0f32270fca1`, with the browser
  tests): 6,172 unit and integration tests, and 126, 2 and 6 browser tests in its three runs, none
  skipped, failed or flaky. 25.5 ticked with P7.4 and P7.5 (the P7 section's two tasks it built).
- The `claims-auditor`, given only the task and Phase 25's Done-when line: DONE in round 1. Its
  notes: the browser test runs with no AI, so the draft in the reply box is covered by unit and
  integration tests only; the "AI down" email test covers no AI at all (a slow or broken AI shares
  the shifts line's tested path).

### Left for later (Parking lot)

- The purge's and erasure's log counts don't count emails or their replies yet; the shifts line's
  failed automatic reply doesn't go back to a person; a casual's reply to our email reaches
  Enquiries but not the firm's own mailbox; drafts are made for every email for a person; the
  database's check doesn't cover a held pay email's draft. The provider (its ADR and DMARC word)
  and the privacy notice's line before any firm's inbox goes live.

## 2026-10-31 · Phase 25.6 (P8): messages in the app

### Dylan's go-ahead

- "just do what you would recommend stop prompting me and build it": the mock-up was published
  for the record (https://claude.ai/artifact/L3a8sGwXo7anqQW6NayYGc) and built as recommended.

### What changed, in plain words

- Casuals write to the office inside the app: a fifth tab, Messages, between Shifts and Pay. Her
  messages and the answers, oldest first; the box "Write to the office" and Send. Nothing goes by
  text ("it would be an internal chat on the app not texts being sent out", Dylan, 2026-10-26).
- Each message is sorted as the shifts line sorts a WhatsApp (a keyword, else the AI's label, else
  the office) and answered in the app at once with the same words and links, whatever the firm's
  automatic replies switch (decision 11: that switch is about texts). At most 5 automatic answers
  an hour to one casual, none to a message quoting "Automatic reply.", none to someone no longer
  active: those wait for a person. At most 20 messages an hour from one casual.
- Anything for a person waits in Enquiries beside WhatsApps, texts and emails (a two-bubble icon,
  "In the app"). The office's reply shows in her app at once, signed by the firm, with a push to
  her phone that holds none of the words; the Messages tab counts replies she hasn't seen.
- Help's last line now points to Messages.
- The `messages` table takes the `app` channel (always the casual's, never a number: database
  checks), and the casual gets the time of the newest office reply she's seen
  (`messages_read_at`), cleared on erasure. Migration 0064.
- Words in `docs/screens.md` ("Messages: the in-app chat") and `docs/messaging-templates.md`
  ("Messages in the app", the push) first.

### Checks

- Tests: the receive step, each answer path, the limits (the 5th answer counted again in the
  claim, the AI not asked past it, the 21st message), an echo, a former casual, the last try, the
  order of one casual's messages, a send repeated on weak signal, the office's reply and its push
  (once only; none for an automatic answer), the thread and the tab's count, erasure, the export,
  and another firm's casual, on the real database; every new line checked against the docs; the
  plain-language check over the new screens; a browser test of the round trip on a phone (casual,
  office, casual).
- The `guardrails-reviewer`: no critical, high or medium finding; safe to commit on the guardrails.
  Fixed from its lows: the hour's count taken again in the claim under a lock on the casual; the
  AI not asked when no answer can go; a send repeated after weak signal stores nothing new (the
  form's own id); erasure clears `messages_read_at`; the tab's count marks read only the replies
  the page showed; the echo line taken from the template, in any case; the push only for the
  office's own reply. Not done (Parking lot): "not confirmed" if the push job's record fails, the
  clock-hour send limit, pay figures typed into a reply.
